# System Control
## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659353"></a>

System Control, based on chip characteristics, completes the reset and basic initialization of each hardware component. It is also responsible for initializing and de-initializing each business module of the MPP (Media Process Platform) system, managing the working status of each MPP system business module, providing the current MPP system version information, and providing large-block physical memory management functions.

Before an application starts MPP services, it must complete MPP system initialization. Similarly, after the application exits MPP services, it must complete MPP system de-initialization to release resources.

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-notice.gif) **Notice:** 
>-   Unless otherwise specified, the descriptions for SS625V100/SS626V100 are consistent with SS528V100, and the descriptions for SS524V100 are consistent with SS522V100.
>-   SS528V100, SS625V100, and SS524V100 do not support low latency, stitching, and MCF

## Important Concepts<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259854"></a>


### Low Latency<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659161"></a>

Low latency means that after the image is written to the specified number of lines to DDR, the front-end module reports an interrupt and sends the image to the back-end module for processing. This can reduce pipeline latency, and the front-end and back-end modules use a specific mechanism to ensure that the image is written before being read, so no image read errors occur.

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-notice.gif) **Notice:** 
>-   Enabling low latency on each module may conflict with certain specific feature combinations. For the specific enabling method and corresponding limitations, refer to the relevant low latency descriptions in each module chapter.
>-   Hi3403V100/SS626V100 support low latency. The supported modules are shown in [Table 1](#_Ref71645661) and [Table 2](#_Toc74057716) respectively. Modules not listed do not support low latency.

## Functional Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408100026"></a>







### Video Buffer Pool<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259890"></a>

The video buffer pool mainly provides large-block physical memory management functions for media services, responsible for memory allocation and recycling, fully leveraging the role of the memory buffer pool to ensure reasonable use of physical memory resources across various media processing modules.

A group of cache blocks of the same size and contiguous physical addresses form a video buffer pool. The public video buffer pool must be configured before system initialization. Depending on the service, the number of public buffer pools and the size and quantity of cache blocks may vary.

All video input channels can obtain video cache blocks from the public video buffer pool to store captured images. As shown in [Figure 1](#fig17183122371915), VI obtains cache block Bm from the public video buffer pool B. Cache block Bm is sent from VI to VPSS. After being processed by VPSS, the input cache block Bm is released back to the public video buffer pool. Assuming the VPSS channel working mode is USER, VPSS channel 0 obtains cache block Bi from public video buffer pool B as the output image cache buffer and sends it to VENC. VPSS channel 1 obtains cache block Bk from public video buffer pool B as the output image cache buffer and sends it to VO. Bi is released back to the public video buffer pool after VENC encoding is complete, and Bk is released back after VO display is complete.

**Figure 1**  Typical Public Video Buffer Pool Data Flow Diagram<a name="fig17183122371915"></a>  
【describe】

For different types of video buffer pool size calculation, see the code in ot\_buffer.h

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-notice.gif) **Notice:** 
>For SS528V100/SS524V100, when the image width is less than or equal to 256 and compact segment compression is used, the memory size obtained through the ot\_common\_get\_pic\_buf\_size interface is equal to the non-compact segment compression size.
>When Hi3403V100 allocates the buffer required for MCF scenarios, if the mono path is not a luminance single-component image, the ot\_vpss\_get\_mcf\_color\_buf\_size must be used to calculate the buffer size.

### System Binding<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099906"></a>

MPP provides the system binding interface ([ss\_mpi\_sys\_bind](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659349)), which establishes an association between a data source and a data receiver by having the receiver bind to the source (only the data receiver is allowed to bind to the data source). After binding, the data generated by the source will be automatically sent to the receiver. The binding relationships currently supported by MPP are shown in [Table 1](#_Ref289523395).

**Table 1**  MPP Supported Binding Relationships

### Forced VB Destruction<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259930"></a>

When VB is in an occupied state, it cannot be destroyed. By adding the module parameter g\_vb\_force\_exit=1 when inserting xx\_base.ko, VB can be forcibly destroyed even when it is in use. Therefore, use this feature with caution. Ensure that VB is not actively destroyed during normal service operation. VB must only be actively destroyed after all services have completely exited.

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-notice.gif) **Notice:** 
>The forced VB destruction feature is designed to facilitate service restart after an application crashes abnormally. After a user-mode application crashes abnormally, the application can no longer follow the normal service exit procedure. Before restarting the service, you must first call [ss\_mpi\_sys\_exit](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699041) and [ss\_mpi\_vb\_exit](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699081) to destroy the VB resources that could not be destroyed after the previous abnormal application crash, and then proceed with the normal service restart process.

### VI and VPSS Working Modes<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699097"></a>

The working modes of VI and VPSS are divided into online and offline modes. The working mode descriptions are shown in [Table 1](#_Ref48202819).

**Table 1**  VI and VPSS Working Mode Description

<a name="_Ref48202819"></a>
<table><thead align="left"><tr id="row3392mcpsimp"><th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="20%" id="mcps1.2.4.1.1"><p id="p3394mcpsimp"><a name="p3394mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3394mcpsimp"></a>Mode</p>
</th>
【describe】
</th>
【describe】
</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr id="row3400mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="20%" headers="mcps1.2.4.1.1 "><p id="p3402mcpsimp"><a name="p3402mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3402mcpsimp"></a>Online Mode</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="39%" headers="mcps1.2.4.1.2 "><p id="p3404mcpsimp"><a name="p3404mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3404mcpsimp"></a>Online data flow transmission between VI_CAP and VI_PROC. In this mode, VI_CAP does not write RAW data to DDR, but directly sends the data stream to VI_PROC.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="41%" headers="mcps1.2.4.1.3 "><p id="p3406mcpsimp"><a name="p3406mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3406mcpsimp"></a>Online data flow transmission between VI_PROC and VPSS. In this mode, VI_PROC does not write YUV data to DDR, but directly sends the data stream to VPSS.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row3407mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="20%" headers="mcps1.2.4.1.1 "><p id="p3409mcpsimp"><a name="p3409mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3409mcpsimp"></a>Offline Mode</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="39%" headers="mcps1.2.4.1.2 "><p id="p3411mcpsimp"><a name="p3411mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3411mcpsimp"></a>VI_CAP writes RAW data to DDR, then VI_PROC reads the RAW data from DDR for post-processing.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="41%" headers="mcps1.2.4.1.3 "><p id="p3413mcpsimp"><a name="p3413mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3413mcpsimp"></a>VI_PROC writes YUV data to DDR, then VPSS reads the YUV data from DDR for post-processing.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

PIPE can be set to multiple working modes, as follows.

-   PIPE 0 can have 4 modes:
    -   VI Online VPSS Offline
    -   VI Online VPSS Online
    -   VI Offline VPSS Offline
    -   VI Offline VPSS Online

-   Other PIPEs can have 2 modes:
    -   When PIPE 0 is in VI Offline VPSS Online mode, other bound PIPEs can only be set to VI Offline VPSS Online.
    -   When PIPE 0 is set to other modes, other bound PIPEs can only be set to VI Offline VPSS Offline.

-   PIPE can switch between different working modes. In this case, VI needs to be recreated, as follows:
    -   VI Online mode cannot switch to VI Offline mode.
    -   VI Offline mode cannot switch to VI Online mode.

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-note.gif) **Note:** 
>-   The "VI Online/Offline" mentioned in this document refers to the online/offline mode between VI\_CAP and VI\_PROC.
>-   The "VPSS Online/Offline" mentioned in this document refers to the online/offline mode between VI\_PROC and VPSS.

For VI PIPE working modes, please see [Table 2](#_Ref48202856).

### VI Video Mode<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699173"></a>

The VI video mode is divided into two types: Normal mode and Advanced mode. The default is Normal mode. On a single linear path, using Advanced mode yields better results. In other scenarios, Normal mode is sufficient. This mode applies to all VI pipes. See the [ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_vi\_video\_mode](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699065) interface description.

### logmpp Logging<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259870"></a>

System runtime log information is recorded in the logmpp file. When abnormal operation occurs, you can troubleshoot by viewing the log file. The view command is cat /dev/logmpp.

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-note.gif) **Note:** 
>For SS626V100, when the decoding channel is deployed on the MDC, the MDC logs are recorded in the logmpp\_mdc file. The view command is cat /dev/logmpp\_mdc. This feature requires the lower 32-bit MMZ address. To disable this feature, add the module parameter g\_mdc\_log\_enable=0 when inserting xx\_base.ko.

## API Reference<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699093"></a>

System Control implements MPP (Media Process Platform) system initialization, system binding/unbinding, obtaining the MPP version number, video buffer pool initialization, and creating video buffer pools.

This functional module provides the following MPI:

-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699005): Configures system control parameters.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699165): Gets system control parameters.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_init](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699113): Initializes the MPP system.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_exit](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699041): De-initializes the MPP system.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_bind](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659349): Binds a data source to a data receiver.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_unbind](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259862): Unbinds a data source from a data receiver.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_bind\_by\_dst](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699177): Gets the bound source based on the destination.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_bind\_by\_src](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259846): Gets the bound destination based on the source.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_version](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259766): Gets the MPP version number.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_chip\_id](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259934): Gets the current chip ID.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_unique\_id](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259882): Gets the current chip unique ID.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_custom\_code](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259794): Gets the current chip custom code.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_cur\_pts](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408100034): Gets the current timestamp.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_init\_pts\_base](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699101): Initializes the MPP timestamp.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_sync\_pts](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259942): Synchronizes the MPP timestamp.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mmap](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259838): Memory storage mapping interface.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mmap\_cached](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659193): Memory storage mapping interface with cache.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_munmap](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408100030): Memory storage unmapping interface.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_flush\_cache](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659301): Flushes cache contents to memory and invalidates cache contents.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mmz\_alloc](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099894): Allocates MMZ memory in user mode.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mmz\_alloc\_cached](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099998): Allocates MMZ memory in user mode. This memory supports cache.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mmz\_free](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259866): Frees MMZ memory in user mode.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_mem\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699181): Sets the DDR name for module device channel memory usage.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_mem\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659261): Gets the MMZ zone name used by the module device channel.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_close\_fd](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259770): Closes all SYS opened logs and system Fds.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_virt\_mem\_info](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699169): Gets the corresponding memory information based on the virtual address.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_scale\_coef\_level](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699117): Sets the scaling coefficient level for the VPSS and VGS modules.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_scale\_coef\_level](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099898): Gets the scaling coefficient level of VPSS and VGS.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_time\_zone](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659321): Sets the time zone information.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_time\_zone](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259878): Gets the time zone information.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_gps\_info](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659325): Sets GPS information.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_gps\_info](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699077): Gets GPS information.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_schedule\_mode](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659145): Sets the system scheduling mode.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_schedule\_mode](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408100022): Gets the system scheduling mode.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_vi\_vpss\_mode](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099934): Sets the working mode of VI and VPSS.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_vi\_vpss\_mode](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099822): Gets the working mode of VI and VPSS.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_vi\_video\_mode](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699065): Sets the VI video mode.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_vi\_video\_mode](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259822): Gets the VI video mode.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_raw\_frame\_compress\_param](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099926): Sets RAW frame compression parameters.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_raw\_frame\_compress\_param](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259834): Gets RAW frame compression parameters.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_tuning\_connect](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441698989): Sets the Tuning tool connection.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_tuning\_connect](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259926): Gets the Tuning tool connection.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mem\_share](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441698965): Shares the mmz buffer corresponding to the handle with a specific process ID.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mem\_unshare](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699053): Removes the sharing of the mmz buffer corresponding to the handle from the process ID.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mem\_share\_all](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259818): Shares the mmz buffer corresponding to the handle with all processes without process ID restriction.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mem\_unshare\_all](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659237): Removes the sharing of the mmz buffer corresponding to the handle from all processes.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_mem\_info\_by\_handle](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659265): Gets the memory description information of the mmz buffer through the handle.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_mem\_info\_by\_phys](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099870): Gets the memory description information of the mmz buffer through the physical address.
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_mem\_info\_by\_virt](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259802): Gets the memory description information of the mmz buffer through the user-mode virtual address.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_set\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659277): Sets MPP video buffer pool attributes.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_get\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699029): Gets MPP video buffer pool attributes.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_init](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659317): Initializes the MPP video buffer pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_exit](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699081): De-initializes the MPP video buffer pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_create\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408100006): Creates a user video buffer pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_create\_ext\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099938): Creates a virtual video buffer pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_destroy\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659173): Destroys a video buffer pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_get\_blk](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699141): Gets a cache block.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_release\_blk](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099958): Releases an acquired cache block.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_insert\_buf\_to\_ext\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659213): Adds a cache block to the virtual video buffer pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_delete\_buf\_from\_ext\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699145): Deletes a cache block from the virtual video buffer pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_phys\_addr\_to\_handle](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099946): Gets the handle of a cache block through its physical address in user mode.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_handle\_to\_phys\_addr](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659329): Gets the physical address of a cache block.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_handle\_to\_pool\_id](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259918): Gets the pool ID of the buffer pool where a cache block resides.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_get\_pool\_info](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259754): Gets the information of a video buffer pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_init\_mod\_common\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259902): Initializes the module public video buffer pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_exit\_mod\_common\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259858): Deregisters the module public video buffer pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_set\_mod\_pool\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099882): Sets the module public video buffer pool attributes.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_get\_mod\_pool\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659189): Gets the module public video buffer pool attributes.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_inquire\_user\_cnt](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699085): Queries cache block usage count information.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_get\_supplement\_addr](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408100010): Gets the supplementary information of the VB block memory.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_set\_supplement\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699157): Sets the supplementary information of VB memory.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_get\_supplement\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099854): Gets the supplementary information of VB memory.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_get\_common\_pool\_id](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099842): Gets the pool ID of the public VB pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_get\_mod\_common\_pool\_id](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699025): Gets the pool ID of the module public VB pool.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_pool\_share](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659313): Shares the VB pool corresponding to the pool ID with a specific process ID.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_pool\_unshare](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659357): Removes the sharing of the VB pool corresponding to the pool ID from the process ID.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_pool\_share\_all](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659233): Shares the VB pool corresponding to the pool ID with all processes without process ID restriction.
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_pool\_unshare\_all](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659345): Removes the sharing of the VB pool corresponding to the pool ID from all processes.
-   [ss\_mpi\_log\_set\_level\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699137): Sets the log level.
-   [ss\_mpi\_log\_get\_level\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659281): Gets the log level.
-   [ss\_mpi\_log\_set\_wait\_flag](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659201): Sets the wait flag when reading logs.
-   [ss\_mpi\_log\_read](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659197): Reads the log.
-   [ss\_mpi\_log\_close](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659273): Closes the log file.

None.

### ss\_mpi\_sys\_init<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699113"></a>

【describe】

Initializes the MPP system. Modules including audio input/output, video input/output, video encoding/decoding, video overlay region, video processing, and graphics processing will be initialized.

**Syntax**

```
td_s32 ss_mpi_sys_init(td_void);
```

**Parameters**

None.

**Return Value**

<a name="table10741mcpsimp"></a>
<table><thead align="left"><tr id="row10746mcpsimp"><th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.1"><p id="p10748mcpsimp"><a name="p10748mcpsimp"></a><a name="p10748mcpsimp"></a>Return Value</p>
</th>
<th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.2"><p id="p10750mcpsimp"><a name="p10750mcpsimp"></a><a name="p10750mcpsimp"></a>Description</p>
</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr id="row10752mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p10754mcpsimp"><a name="p10754mcpsimp"></a><a name="p10754mcpsimp"></a>0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p10756mcpsimp"><a name="p10756mcpsimp"></a><a name="p10756mcpsimp"></a>Success.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row10757mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p10759mcpsimp"><a name="p10759mcpsimp"></a><a name="p10759mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p10761mcpsimp"><a name="p10761mcpsimp"></a><a name="p10761mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <span xml:lang="fr-FR" id="ph10763mcpsimp"><a name="ph10763mcpsimp"></a><a name="ph10763mcpsimp"></a>Error Code</span></a>.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Since the normal operation of the MPP system depends on the buffer pool, you must first call [ss\_mpi\_vb\_init](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659317) to initialize the buffer pool before initializing the MPP system. Otherwise, service operation will be abnormal.
-   If initialized multiple times, success will still be returned, but the system will not have any impact on the MPP running status.
-   Only one process needs to perform initialization; not all processes need to do system initialization.
-   Since the audio module depends on user-mode attributes, audio does not support multi-process operations. Users must ensure that audio-related operations and [ss\_mpi\_vb\_init](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659317) are in the same process.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_exit](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699081)

### ss\_mpi\_sys\_exit<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699041"></a>

【describe】

De-initializes the MPP system. Modules including audio input/output, video input/output, video encoding/decoding, video overlay region, video processing, and graphics processing will be destroyed or disabled.

**Syntax**

```
td_s32 ss_mpi_sys_exit(td_void);
```

**Parameters**

None.

**Return Value**

<a name="table11122mcpsimp"></a>
<table><thead align="left"><tr id="row11127mcpsimp"><th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.1"><p id="p11129mcpsimp"><a name="p11129mcpsimp"></a><a name="p11129mcpsimp"></a>Return Value</p>
</th>
<th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.2"><p id="p11131mcpsimp"><a name="p11131mcpsimp"></a><a name="p11131mcpsimp"></a>Description</p>
</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr id="row11133mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p11135mcpsimp"><a name="p11135mcpsimp"></a><a name="p11135mcpsimp"></a>0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p11137mcpsimp"><a name="p11137mcpsimp"></a><a name="p11137mcpsimp"></a>Success.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row11138mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p11140mcpsimp"><a name="p11140mcpsimp"></a><a name="p11140mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p11142mcpsimp"><a name="p11142mcpsimp"></a><a name="p11142mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <span xml:lang="fr-FR" id="ph11144mcpsimp"><a name="ph11144mcpsimp"></a><a name="ph11144mcpsimp"></a>Error Code</span></a>.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   During de-initialization, if there is a user process blocked on MPI, calling ss\_mpi\_sys\_exit will wake up the blocked process. If the wake-up is unsuccessful, de-initialization will fail. If all blocked MPI calls return, de-initialization can proceed successfully.
-   De-initialization can be performed repeatedly and will not return failure.
-   Since system de-initialization does not destroy audio encoding/decoding channels, the destruction of these channels must be performed by the user. If the process that created these channels exits, the channels will be destroyed accordingly.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_init](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659317)

<tr id="row8578mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p8580mcpsimp"><a name="p8580mcpsimp"></a><a name="p8580mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p8582mcpsimp"><a name="p8582mcpsimp"></a><a name="p8582mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <span xml:lang="fr-FR" id="ph8584mcpsimp"><a name="ph8584mcpsimp"></a><a name="ph8584mcpsimp"></a>Error Code</span></a>.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   The binding relationships currently supported by the system, see Table 1.
-   A data receiver can only be bound to one data source.
-   Binding refers to establishing an association between a data source and a data receiver. After binding, the data generated by the data source will be automatically sent to the receiver.
-   When VDEC acts as a data source, it uses the channel as the sender to send data to other modules. The user sets the device number to 0, and the SDK does not check the input Device number.
-   For SS528V100/SS625V100/SS524V100/SS522V101/SS626V100, when VI acts as a data source, it uses the channel as the sender to send data to other modules. The user sets the device number to 0, and the SDK does not check the input Device number.
-   For Hi3403V100, when VI acts as a data source, it uses the device (pipe) and channel (chn) as the sender to send data to other modules. When acting as a data receiver, it uses the device (pipe) and channel (chn) as the receiver.
-   When VO acts as a data source to send write-back (WBC) data, it uses the device as the sender to send data to other modules. The user sets the Channel number to 0, and the SDK does not check the input Channel number.
-   When VPSS acts as a data receiver, it uses the device (GROUP) as the receiver to receive data from other modules. The user sets the Channel number to 0.
-   When VENC acts as a data receiver, it uses the Channel number as the receiver to receive data from other modules. The user sets the device number to 0, and the SDK does not check the input Device number. If VENC operates in OT\_VENC\_PIC\_RECV\_MULTI mode, the user needs to configure the device number, which is actually used to specify the input source. The macros OT\_VENC\_RECV\_SRC0, OT\_VENC\_RECV\_SRC1, OT\_VENC\_RECV\_SRC2, and OT\_VENC\_RECV\_SRC3 can be used to specify the input source.
-   When AVS acts as a data receiver, it uses the device (GROUP) and channel (PIPE) as the receiver.
-   When MCF acts as a data receiver, it uses the device (GROUP) and channel (PIPE) as the receiver.
-   In other cases, the device number and Channel number must be specified.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_sys\_unbind](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259862)

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19%" headers="mcps1.1.4.1.3 "><p id="p7549mcpsimp"><a name="p7549mcpsimp"></a><a name="p7549mcpsimp"></a>Input</p>

**Return Value**

<a name="table7551mcpsimp"></a>
<table><thead align="left"><tr id="row7556mcpsimp"><th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.1"><p id="p7558mcpsimp"><a name="p7558mcpsimp"></a><a name="p7558mcpsimp"></a>Return Value</p>
</th>
<th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.2"><p id="p7560mcpsimp"><a name="p7560mcpsimp"></a><a name="p7560mcpsimp"></a>Description</p>
</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr id="row7562mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p7564mcpsimp"><a name="p7564mcpsimp"></a><a name="p7564mcpsimp"></a>0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p7566mcpsimp"><a name="p7566mcpsimp"></a><a name="p7566mcpsimp"></a>Success.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row7567mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p7569mcpsimp"><a name="p7569mcpsimp"></a><a name="p7569mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p7571mcpsimp"><a name="p7571mcpsimp"></a><a name="p7571mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <span xml:lang="fr-FR" id="ph7573mcpsimp"><a name="ph7573mcpsimp"></a><a name="ph7573mcpsimp"></a>Error Code</span></a>.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

Initializing the timestamp base will forcibly set the current system timestamp to pts\_base, with no constraints related to the original system timestamp. Therefore, it is recommended to call this interface when media services have not started (e.g., right after the operating system boots). If media services have already started, it is recommended to call [ss\_mpi\_sys\_sync\_pts](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259942) for timestamp fine-tuning.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

None.

</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr id="row9509mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="43%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p9511mcpsimp"><a name="p9511mcpsimp"></a><a name="p9511mcpsimp"></a>0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="56.99999999999999%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p9513mcpsimp"><a name="p9513mcpsimp"></a><a name="p9513mcpsimp"></a>Success.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row9514mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="43%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p9516mcpsimp"><a name="p9516mcpsimp"></a><a name="p9516mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="56.99999999999999%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p9518mcpsimp"><a name="p9518mcpsimp"></a><a name="p9518mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <span xml:lang="fr-FR" id="ph9520mcpsimp"><a name="ph9520mcpsimp"></a><a name="ph9520mcpsimp"></a>Error Code</span></a>.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

Fine-tunes the current system timestamp (microsecond level). After adjustment, timestamp rollback will not occur. When synchronizing between multiple chips, since the clock source error of the board may be relatively large, it is recommended to perform timestamp fine-tuning once per second.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

None.

</tr>
</tbody>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   The opened node is dev/mmz\_userdev, which implements mapping of non-device type (non-cacheable, reorderable) DDR addresses in MMZ.
-   For the input address and size, they must be within the address range of a single (allocated) mmz buffer.
-   The corresponding unmapping interface is [ss\_mpi\_sys\_munmap](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408100030).

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_sys\_munmap](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408100030)

</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row5362mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p5364mcpsimp"><a name="p5364mcpsimp"></a><a name="p5364mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p5366mcpsimp"><a name="p5366mcpsimp"></a><a name="p5366mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <span xml:lang="fr-FR" id="ph5368mcpsimp"><a name="ph5368mcpsimp"></a><a name="ph5368mcpsimp"></a>Error Code</span></a>.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   When the data in the cache is the latest data, to ensure that hardware that cannot directly access the cache (such as ive) can obtain correct data when accessing memory, this interface must be called first to update the cache contents to memory. This ensures data consistency and correctness when the hardware accesses memory.
-   This interface should be used together with the [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mmap\_cached](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659193) or [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mmz\_alloc\_cached](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099998) interface.
-   The user needs to ensure the validity of the input parameters.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_sys\_mmap\_cached](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659193)

<tr id="row6711mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p6713mcpsimp"><a name="p6713mcpsimp"></a><a name="p6713mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p6715mcpsimp"><a name="p6715mcpsimp"></a><a name="p6715mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <span xml:lang="fr-FR" id="ph6717mcpsimp"><a name="ph6717mcpsimp"></a><a name="ph6717mcpsimp"></a>Error Code</span></a>.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

MMZ is divided into multiple zones (Zone). Each zone contains multiple Mmbs. This interface is called to allocate a memory block named \*mmb in the MMZ zone named \*zone, with the size len, and returns the physical address and user-mode virtual address pointer. If there is a zone named "anonymous" in MMZ, \*zone can be set to NULL. If \*mmb is set to NULL, the created Mmb block is named "<null>".

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

None.

<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p8678mcpsimp"><a name="p8678mcpsimp"></a><a name="p8678mcpsimp"></a>Success.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row8679mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p8681mcpsimp"><a name="p8681mcpsimp"></a><a name="p8681mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p8683mcpsimp"><a name="p8683mcpsimp"></a><a name="p8683mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <span xml:lang="fr-FR" id="ph8685mcpsimp"><a name="ph8685mcpsimp"></a><a name="ph8685mcpsimp"></a>Error Code</span></a>.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   The difference between this interface and the [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mmz\_alloc](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099894) interface: memory allocated through this interface supports cache. For frequently used memory, it is best to use this interface to allocate memory, which can improve CPU read/write efficiency and enhance system performance. For example, when users use ive operators, there is a large amount of data being frequently read and written. Using this interface to allocate memory can effectively improve CPU efficiency.
-   When the CPU accesses memory allocated by this interface, the data in memory is placed in the cache. However, hardware devices (such as ive) can only access physical memory and cannot access the cache content. For memory that is operated on by both the CPU and hardware, [ss\_mpi\_sys\_flush\_cache](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659301) must be called to ensure data synchronization.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mmz\_alloc](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099894)
-   [ss\_mpi\_sys\_flush\_cache](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659301)

<tr id="row7854mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p7856mcpsimp"><a name="p7856mcpsimp"></a><a name="p7856mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p7858mcpsimp"><a name="p7858mcpsimp"></a><a name="p7858mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <span xml:lang="fr-FR" id="ph7860mcpsimp"><a name="ph7860mcpsimp"></a><a name="ph7860mcpsimp"></a>Error Code</span></a>.</p>
</td>

</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Unless otherwise specified, the memory parameters are configured according to the corresponding channel of the module.
-   If the input MMZ zone name does not exist, the interface will still return success, but memory allocation will fail during service operation. Therefore, to ensure correct memory allocation, the input MMZ zone name must be selected from the names defined during MMZ zone partitioning.
-   When applying an equal memory distribution strategy for the VO module, the user must ensure that the channel memories under the same device number are on the same DDR. If inconsistent, the SDK defaults to allocating memory from the DDR set by channel 0 of this device.
-   For the VFMW module, the shared MMZ memory used by all channels is configured through vfmw device 0 channel 0, including vfmw\(%d\)\_vdh\_msg, vfmw\_vdh\_ext, vfmw\_scd\_msg, and vfmw\_mdma\_msg. The MMZ memory used independently by each channel is configured through the corresponding vdec channel.
-   The vedu\_hal memory of the VENC module is configured through venc device 0 channel 0. Other mmz memories are configured through the corresponding venc channel.
-   The IVE module does not support accessing space exceeding 4G. Please ensure that the IVE input and output memories are in the IVE corresponding MMZ zone area. Otherwise, address verification may fail. If an MMZ zone is specified for IVE access, only the space from the specified zone's MMZ start address to (4G - 1) can be accessed. If not specified, the accessible MMZ address space is from the MMZ anonymous start address to (4G - 1).
-   For the VDEC\_ADAPT module, the vdec\_adp\_proc and vdec\_adp\_vb memories are configured through channel 0. Other mmz memories are configured according to the corresponding channel.
-   For the DCC module, the dcc\_msg\_buf memory is configured through device 0 channel 0.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_sys\_get\_mem\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659261)

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p6267mcpsimp"><a name="p6267mcpsimp"></a><a name="p6267mcpsimp"></a>Success.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row6268mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p6270mcpsimp"><a name="p6270mcpsimp"></a><a name="p6270mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p6272mcpsimp"><a name="p6272mcpsimp"></a><a name="p6272mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <span xml:lang="fr-FR" id="ph6274mcpsimp"><a name="ph6274mcpsimp"></a><a name="ph6274mcpsimp"></a>Error Code</span></a>.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   The default value of the scaling coefficient level for each scaling ratio can be found in the Notes of the [ot\_scale\_coef\_level](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699105) structure.
-   When the scaling coefficient level is increased while keeping the scaling ratio range unchanged, the image within that scaling ratio range will appear sharper. Conversely, decreasing it will make the image blurrier.
-   When the scaling coefficient level is increased while keeping the scaling ratio range unchanged, the degree of aliasing in the image will become more severe. Users can choose according to their needs.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

None.

</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   SS528V100/SS524V100Not supportedthis interface。
-   Reserved values ​​are currently treated as 0.
-   You can set it to 0 or 1 when setting it for the first time. In any other case, no matter what value is set, it will be set to 0 internally.
-   Default value1 is restored after SYS exits.
-   When connect is 1, Tuning tools such as PQTool can be accessed normally. When connect is 0, Tuning tools such as PQTool cannot be accessed.

**Example**

none

**Related Topics**

none

### ss\_mpi\_sys\_mem\_share<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441698965"></a>

【describe】

Share the handlecorresponding mmz buffer to a specific process ID.

**Syntax**

```
td_s32 ss_mpi_sys_mem_share(const td_void *mem_handle, td_s32 pid);
```

**Parameters**

<a name="table3797mcpsimp"></a>
<table><thead align="left"><tr id="row3803mcpsimp"><th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="25%" id="mcps1.1.4.1.1"><p id="p3805mcpsimp"><a name="p3805mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3805mcpsimp"></a>Parameter Name</p>

<tr id="row04628172437"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="25%" headers="mcps1.1.4.1.1 "><p id="p15463717134320"><a name="p15463717134320"></a><a name="p15463717134320"></a>pid</p>
</td>
Only the process that allocates mmz buffer can register the share, and other processes registering the share returns failure.
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="21.52%" headers="mcps1.1.4.1.3 "><p id="p64633172432"><a name="p64633172432"></a><a name="p64633172432"></a>Input</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Return Value**

<a name="table3819mcpsimp"></a>

<th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.2"><p id="p3828mcpsimp"><a name="p3828mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3828mcpsimp"></a>Description</p>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p3834mcpsimp"><a name="p3834mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3834mcpsimp"></a>Success.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row3835mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p3837mcpsimp"><a name="p3837mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3837mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p3839mcpsimp"><a name="p3839mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3839mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <a href="#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659157">Error Code</a>。</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Only the process that allocates mmz buffer can register the share, and other processes registering the share returns failure.
-   The process that allocates mmz buffer does not need to be shared. If the pid parameter is the process ID that allocates mmz buffer, the interface returns failure.
-   If the mmz buffer is already shared by the whole process, the interface returns failure.
-   One mmz buffer can allow up to 5 process IDs to be shared (including the process ID that allocates the mmz buffer).
-   If shared repeatedly to the same process ID, the interface returns Success.
-   If setMMZModule Parametermem\_process\_isolation=0, this interface will not take effect even if it returns successfully.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_sys\_mem\_unshare](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699053)

```

<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p147071944105610"><a name="p147071944105610"></a><a name="p147071944105610"></a>Success.</p>

<tr id="row5708644125619"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p5708124419562"><a name="p5708124419562"></a><a name="p5708124419562"></a>Non-0</p>

</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   The process that allocates mmz buffer can unshare the process ID of the non-allocated mmz buffer.
-   A shared process can only unshare its own process ID.
-   If the mmz buffer is already shared by the whole process, the interface returns failure.
-   Repeatedly unsharing the process ID, the interface returns failure.
-   Paired with the [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mem\_share](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441698965) interface.
-   If setMMZModule Parametermem\_process\_isolation=0, this interface will not take effect even if it returns successfully.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_sys\_mem\_share](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441698965)

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Only the process that allocated mmz buffer can unshare it to all processes.
-   Repeatedly release sharing for all processes, and the interface returns Success.
-   Paired with the [ss\_mpi\_sys\_mem\_share\_all](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259818) interface.
-   After calling this interface to release the shared state for all processes, the original shared state of the process ID is still retained.
-   If setMMZModule Parametermem\_process\_isolation=0, this interface will not take effect even if it returns successfully.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_sys\_mem\_share\_all](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259818)

<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p87477620422"><a name="p87477620422"></a><a name="p87477620422"></a>Success.</p>

<tr id="row97477694211"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p67479684212"><a name="p67479684212"></a><a name="p67479684212"></a>Non-0</p>

<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p97471668429"><a name="p97471668429"></a><a name="p97471668429"></a>On failure, see the error code.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Only the process that allocates mmz buffer and the shared process can obtain the memory description information of mmz buffer through handle.
-   In the memory description information obtained by this interface, the offset value is fixed at 0.
-   If setMMZModule Parametermem\_process\_isolation=0, the memory description information of mmz buffer can be obtained if the parameters are legal.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Only the process that allocates the mmz buffer and the shared process can obtain the memory description information of the mmz buffer through the physical address.
-   If setMMZModule Parametermem\_process\_isolation=0, the memory description information of mmz buffer can be obtained if the parameters are legal.

**Example**

None.

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_vb.h、ss\_mpi\_vb.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   The cache pool properties can only be set when the system is not initialized, otherwise failure will be returned.
-   Video buf requires different configurations according to different application scenarios. For configuration rules, see [Video Cache Pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259890).
-   The size of each cache block in the common cache pool should vary depending on the current image pixel format and whether the image is compressed. The specific allocation size is described in the See[ot\_vb\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659361) structure.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_get\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699029)

**Note**

-   You must first call [ss\_mpi\_sys\_exit](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699041) to the InitializationMPP system, and then to the Initialization cache pool, otherwise it returns failure.
-   You can go to Initialization repeatedly without returning failure.
-   Going to Initialization will not clear the previous configuration of the cache pool.
-   Before exiting the VB pool, please make sure that no VB in the VB pool is occupied, otherwise you cannot exit.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_init](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659317)

</td>

</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_vb.h、ss\_mpi\_vb.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   The cache pool created by this interface is empty. VB is not allocated when the pool is created, but comes from outside and is allocated by an external program.
-   Users cannot obtain cache blocks from the pool.
-   The virtual video cache pool cannot be used as the user VB pool of the module, otherwise the module will not be able to obtain VB.
-   Among the vb\_pool\_cfg parameters, only blk\_size and blk\_cnt are valid and need to be configured. Other members are reserved and do not need to be configured. The interface will not be checked.
-   Users can call [ss\_mpi\_vb\_destroy\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659173) to destroy the cache pool. When destroying, the user must ensure that there are no actual cache blocks in the cache pool.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_insert\_buf\_to\_ext\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659213)
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_delete\_buf\_from\_ext\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699145)
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_destroy\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659173)

</tbody>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_vb.h、ss\_mpi\_vb.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Destroying a non-existent buffer pool returns OT\_ERR\_VB\_UNEXIST.
-   When going to the InitializationMPP cache pool, all cache pools will be destroyed, including user-mode cache pools.
-   Before exiting the VB pool, please make sure that no VB in the VB pool is occupied, otherwise you cannot exit.
-   The cache pool ID number within the range of \[0, [OT\_VB\_MAX\_POOLS](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259898)\), including the ID numbers of public cache pools, module public cache pools, module private cache pools, etc. Please ensure that the Pool is the ID number of the cache pool created by [ss\_mpi\_vb\_create\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408100006), otherwise failure will be returned.
-   The operations of destroying a VB pool and creating a VB pool (or creating a virtual VB pool) need to be performed in the same process.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_create\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408100006)

<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p1621mcpsimp"><a name="p1621mcpsimp"></a><a name="p1621mcpsimp"></a>A valid cache block handle. </p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row1622mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p1624mcpsimp"><a name="p1624mcpsimp"></a><a name="p1624mcpsimp"></a>OT_VB_INVALID_HANDLE</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p1626mcpsimp"><a name="p1626mcpsimp"></a><a name="p1626mcpsimp"></a>Get cache block Failure.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Error Codes**

None.

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_vb.h、ss\_mpi\_vb.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   After creating a cache pool, the user can call this interface to obtain a cache block from the cache pool; that is, the first parameter Poolset to is the cache pool ID created; the second parameter blk\_size must be less than or equal to the cache block size specified when creating the cache pool. The parameter mmz\_name is invalid when retrieving cache blocks from the specified cache pool.
-   If the user needs to obtain a cache block of a specified size from any public cache pool, the first parameter Poolset to the invalid ID number (OT\_VB\_INVALID\_POOL\_ID), the second parameter blk\_sizeset to the required cache block size, and specify which public cache pool on the DDR to obtain the cache block from. If there is no public cache pool on the specified DDR, the cache block will not be obtained. If mmz\_name equals NULL, it means that the cache block is obtained from the public cache pool on the unnamed DDR.
-   The public cache pool is mainly used to store VIU captured images. Therefore, improper operations on the public cache pool (such as occupying too many cache blocks) will affect the normal operation of the MPP system.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_release\_blk](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099958)

### ss\_mpi\_vb\_insert\_buf\_to\_ext\_pool<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659213"></a>

【describe】

Add a cache block to the virtual video cache pool. This cache block is allocated by the user and is not in the public cache pool.

**Syntax**

```
ot_vb_blk ss_mpi_vb_insert_buf_to_ext_pool(ot_vb_pool pool, td_phys_addr_t phys_addr, td_u64 size, td_bool is_filled);
```

**Parameters**

</tr>
<tr id="row2147mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="25%" headers="mcps1.1.4.1.1 "><p id="p2149mcpsimp"><a name="p2149mcpsimp"></a><a name="p2149mcpsimp"></a>phys_addr</p>

<p id="p2152mcpsimp"><a name="p2152mcpsimp"></a><a name="p2152mcpsimp"></a>Valid range: Valid MMZ physical address, 256-byte aligned. </p>

<tr id="row2155mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="25%" headers="mcps1.1.4.1.1 "><p id="p2157mcpsimp"><a name="p2157mcpsimp"></a><a name="p2157mcpsimp"></a>size</p>
</td>
Cache block size. </p>
<p id="p2160mcpsimp"><a name="p2160mcpsimp"></a><a name="p2160mcpsimp"></a>Valid range: cannot exceed 4GB, in bytes. </p>

<a name="table2171mcpsimp"></a>

</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr id="row2182mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p2184mcpsimp"><a name="p2184mcpsimp"></a><a name="p2184mcpsimp"></a>non-OT_VB_INVALID_HANDLE</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p2186mcpsimp"><a name="p2186mcpsimp"></a><a name="p2186mcpsimp"></a>A valid cache block handle. </p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row2187mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p2189mcpsimp"><a name="p2189mcpsimp"></a><a name="p2189mcpsimp"></a>OT_VB_INVALID_HANDLE</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p2191mcpsimp"><a name="p2191mcpsimp"></a><a name="p2191mcpsimp"></a>Get cache block Failure.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_vb.h、ss\_mpi\_vb.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Cache block memory is allocated and managed by the user himself. This interface just adds cache blocks to the virtual video cache pool.
-   pool must be the ID number of the virtual video cache pool.
-   The size of the cache block must be consistent with the blk\_size specified when creating the virtual video cache pool.
-   After the cache block is added to the virtual video cache pool, [ss\_mpi\_vb\_get\_blk](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699141) can no longer be called to obtain the cache block.
-   The physical address passed by the user needs to be within the valid mmz address range and needs to be aligned to 256 bytes. The interface will check whether there is an address conflict.
-   The physical address of the cache block in the virtual video cache pool is not mapped to a virtual address. If the user needs to use a virtual address, please call the map function to map it yourself.
-   Users can call [ss\_mpi\_vb\_delete\_buf\_from\_ext\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699145) to release cache blocks from the cache pool. After being released, the cache block is no longer in the cache pool. If the user needs to use it again, he needs to call this interface again to add the cache block to the cache pool.
-   Users can call [ss\_mpi\_vb\_inquire\_user\_cnt](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699085) to query whether this cache block is occupied.
-   The user's operations of adding cache blocks and creating a virtual VB pool need to be performed in the same process.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_create\_ext\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099938)
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_delete\_buf\_from\_ext\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699145)

The user releases a cache block from the virtual video cache pool.

### ss\_mpi\_vb\_handle\_to\_pool\_id<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259918"></a>

【describe】

User mode obtains the ID of the buffer pool where a frame buffer block is located.

**Syntax**

```
ot_vb_pool ss_mpi_vb_handle_to_pool_id(ot_vb_blk vb_blk);
```

<tr id="row9270mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p9272mcpsimp"><a name="p9272mcpsimp"></a><a name="p9272mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>

</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_vb.h、ss\_mpi\_vb.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Currently the public video buffer pool is only available for VDEC modules.
-   You must first call [ss\_mpi\_vb\_init](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659317) for public video buffer pool Initialization.
-   You must first call [ss\_mpi\_vb\_set\_mod\_pool\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099882) to configure the cache pool attributes, and then Initialize the cache pool, otherwise it will fail.
-   Initialization can be repeated without returning failure.
-   The VDEC module public pool only needs to be created when the decoding frame memory allocation method uses the module public VB pool.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_exit\_mod\_common\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259858)

</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_vb.h、ss\_mpi\_vb.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   This interface must be called before at [ss\_mpi\_vb\_exit](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699081), otherwise it returns failure.
-   Can exit repeatedly without returning failure.
-   After exiting, the previous configuration of the module's public video cache pool will be cleared.
-   Before exiting the VB pool, please make sure that no VB in the VB pool is occupied, otherwise you cannot exit.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_init\_mod\_common\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259902)

<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="21.21212121212121%" headers="mcps1.1.4.1.3 "><p id="p3058mcpsimp"><a name="p3058mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3058mcpsimp"></a>Input</p>

**Return Value**

<a name="table3060mcpsimp"></a>
<table><thead align="left"><tr id="row3065mcpsimp"><th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.1"><p id="p3067mcpsimp"><a name="p3067mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3067mcpsimp"></a>Return Value</p>
</th>
<th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.2"><p id="p3069mcpsimp"><a name="p3069mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3069mcpsimp"></a>Description</p>
</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr id="row3071mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p3073mcpsimp"><a name="p3073mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3073mcpsimp"></a>0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p3075mcpsimp"><a name="p3075mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3075mcpsimp"></a>Success.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row3076mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p3078mcpsimp"><a name="p3078mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3078mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p3080mcpsimp"><a name="p3080mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3080mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see <span xml:lang="fr-FR" id="ph3082mcpsimp"><a name="ph3082mcpsimp"></a><a name="ph3082mcpsimp"></a>Error Code</span></a>.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_vb.h、ss\_mpi\_vb.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Currently, there are five types of VB memory additional information supported:
-   DCF information, corresponding to the structure ot\_jpeg\_dcf. The corresponding MASK is OT\_VB\_SUPPLEMENT\_JPEG\_MASK.
-   Image motion information. There is no corresponding structure, and the corresponding MASK is OT\_VB\_SUPPLEMENT\_MOTION\_DATA\_MASK.
-   DNG information, the corresponding structure is ot\_dng\_image\_dynamic\_info, and the corresponding MASK is OT\_VB\_SUPPLEMENT\_DNG\_MASK.
-   Video extension supplementary information. The corresponding structure is ot\_video\_supplement\_misc, the corresponding MASK is OT\_VB\_SUPPLEMENT\_MISC\_MASK, and the Hi3403V100 configuration is invalid.
-   BNR information. There is no corresponding structure. The corresponding MASK is OT\_VB\_SUPPLEMENT\_BNR\_MOT\_MASK.

-   This interface needs to be called before ss\_mpi\_vb\_init for the auxiliary information to take effect.
-   SS528V100/SS524V100/SS626V100Not supportedthis interface。

**Example**

none

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_get\_supplement\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099854)

### ss\_mpi\_vb\_pool\_share<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659313"></a>

【describe】

Pool ID corresponding VB pool share to a specific process ID.

**Syntax**

```
td_s32 ss_mpi_vb_pool_share(ot_vb_pool pool, td_s32 pid);
```

**Parameters**

<a name="table3797mcpsimp"></a>
<table><thead align="left"><tr id="row3803mcpsimp"><th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="25%" id="mcps1.1.4.1.1"><p id="p3805mcpsimp"><a name="p3805mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3805mcpsimp"></a>Parameter Name</p>
</th>
<th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="52.980000000000004%" id="mcps1.1.4.1.2"><p id="p3807mcpsimp"><a name="p3807mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3807mcpsimp"></a>Description</p>
</th>
<th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.02%" id="mcps1.1.4.1.3"><p id="p3809mcpsimp"><a name="p3809mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3809mcpsimp"></a>Input/Output</p>
</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr id="row3811mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="25%" headers="mcps1.1.4.1.1 "><p xml:lang="fr-FR" id="p3813mcpsimp"><a name="p3813mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3813mcpsimp"></a>pool</p>
</td>
Only the process that created the VB pool can register the share, and other processes registering the share returns failure.
<p id="p38574134101"><a name="p38574134101"></a><a name="p38574134101"></a>Valid range: [0, OT_VB_MAX_POOLS)</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.02%" headers="mcps1.1.4.1.3 "><p id="p3817mcpsimp"><a name="p3817mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3817mcpsimp"></a>Input</p>
</td>
</tr>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="52.980000000000004%" headers="mcps1.1.4.1.2 "><p id="p84639174433"><a name="p84639174433"></a><a name="p84639174433"></a>Process ID</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.02%" headers="mcps1.1.4.1.3 "><p id="p64633172432"><a name="p64633172432"></a><a name="p64633172432"></a>Input</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Return Value**

<a name="table3819mcpsimp"></a>
<table><thead align="left"><tr id="row3824mcpsimp"><th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.1"><p id="p3826mcpsimp"><a name="p3826mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3826mcpsimp"></a>Return Value</p>
</th>
<th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.2"><p id="p3828mcpsimp"><a name="p3828mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3828mcpsimp"></a>Description</p>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p3834mcpsimp"><a name="p3834mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3834mcpsimp"></a>Success.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row3835mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p3837mcpsimp"><a name="p3837mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3837mcpsimp"></a>Non-0</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p3839mcpsimp"><a name="p3839mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3839mcpsimp"></a>On failure, see the error code.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_vb.h、ss\_mpi\_vb.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Only the process that created the VB pool can register the share, and other processes registering the share returns failure.
-   The process that creates the VB pool does not need to be shared. If the pid parameter is the process ID that created the VB pool, the interface returns failure.
-   If the VB pool is already shared by the whole process, the interface returns failure.
-   A VB pool can allow up to 5 process IDs to be shared (including the process ID that created the VB pool).
-   If shared repeatedly to the same process ID, the interface returns Success.
-   If the cache block of the VB pool is shared with the process ID, it is equivalent to sharing the VB pool with the process ID.
-   Not supported virtual VB pool.
-   If setMMZModule Parametermem\_process\_isolation=0, this interface will not take effect even if it returns successfully.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_pool\_unshare](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659357)

</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row5708644125619"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="50%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p5708124419562"><a name="p5708124419562"></a><a name="p5708124419562"></a>Non-0</p>

</td>

</tbody>
</table>

**Requirements**

-   Header: ot\_common\_vb.h、ss\_mpi\_vb.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   The process that created the VB pool can unshare the ID of the process that did not create the VB pool.
-   A shared process can only unshare its own process ID.
-   If the VB pool is already shared by the whole process, the interface returns failure.
-   Repeatedly unsharing the process ID, the interface returns failure.
-   If the cache block of the VB pool is used to release the sharing of the process ID, it is equivalent to the VB pool releasing the sharing of the process ID.
-   Paired with the [ss\_mpi\_vb\_pool\_share](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659313) interface.
-   Not supported virtual VB pool.
-   If setMMZModule Parametermem\_process\_isolation=0, this interface will not take effect even if it returns successfully.

**Example**

None.

**Related Topics**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_pool\_share](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659313)

-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   Only the process that created the VB pool can unshare it to all processes.
-   Repeatedly release sharing for all processes, and the interface returns Success.
-   Paired with the [ss\_mpi\_vb\_pool\_share\_all](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659233) interface.
-   After calling this interface to release the shared state for all processes, the original shared state of the process ID is still retained.
-   Not supported virtual VB pool.
-   If setMMZModule Parametermem\_process\_isolation=0, this interface will not take effect even if it returns successfully.

None.

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_pool\_share\_all](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659233)

### ss\_mpi\_log\_set\_level\_cfg<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699137"></a>

【describe】

Set the log level.

**Syntax**

```
td_s32 ss_mpi_log_set_level_cfg(ot_log_level_cfg *level_cfg);
```

-   Header: ot\_common\_sys.h、ss\_mpi\_sys.h、ot\_debug.h
-   Library: libss\_mpi.a

**Note**

-   When the member mod\_nameset in level\_cfg is set to the string "all", the log level of all modules will be set. Otherwise, only the log level of the module specified by mod\_id is set.
-   You can only set the log level of the chip Supported module, otherwise it will fail.

None.

#### Public data type<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699001"></a>

```
typedef unsigned char           td_uchar;
typedef unsigned char           td_u8;
typedef unsigned short          td_u16;
typedef unsigned int            td_u32;
typedef unsigned long           td_ulong;
typedef char                    td_char;
typedef signed char            td_s8;
typedef short                   td_s16;
typedef int                     td_s32;
typedef long                    td_slong;
typedef float                   td_float;
typedef double                  td_double;
typedef void                    td_void;
#ifndef _M_IX86
    typedef unsigned long long  td_u64;
    typedef long long           td_s64;
#else
    typedef unsigned __int64  td_u64;
    typedef __int64             td_s64;
#endif
typedef unsigned long int     td_phys_addr_t;
typedef td_u32                  td_handle;
typedef unsigned int           td_fr32;
typedef td_u32 				  ot_vb_pool;
typedef td_u32                  ot_vb_blk;
typedef enum {
    TD_FALSE = 0,
    TD_TRUE  = 1,
} td_bool;
#ifndef NULL
    #define NULL    0L
#endif
#define TD_NULL     0L
#define TD_SUCCESS  0
#define TD_FAILURE  (-1)
typedef td_s32 ot_ai_chn;
typedef td_s32 ot_ao_chn;

typedef td_s32 ot_vdec_chn;
typedef td_s32 ot_ive_handle;
typedef td_s32 ot_fd_chn;
typedef td_s32 ot_md_chn;

typedef td_s32 ot_blk_dev;

/* Maximum number of public video cache pools */
#define OT_INVALID_CHN (-1)
#define OT_INVALID_WAY (-1)
#define OT_INVALID_LAYER (-1)
#define OT_INVALID_DEV (-1)
#define OT_INVALID_HANDLE (-1)
#define OT_INVALID_VALUE (-1)
#define OT_INVALID_TYPE (-1)
#define OT_VB_INVALID_POOL_ID             (-1U)
#define OT_VB_INVALID_HANDLE              (-1U)
/* Maximum number of public video cache pools */
#define OT_VB_MAX_COMMON_POOLS              16
/* Maximum number of module video cache pools */
#define OT_VB_MAX_MOD_COMMON_POOLS          16
/* Owner id of the public video cache pool */
#define OT_POOL_OWNER_COMMON	-1
/* Owner id of private video cache pool */
#define OT_POOL_OWNER_PRIVATE	-2
/* Owner id of the module video cache pool */
#define OT_POOL_OWNER_MODULE	-3
/* Owner id of user video cache pool */
#define OT_POOL_OWNER_USER  -4
/* VB additional information JPEG DCF MASK */
#define OT_VB_SUPPLEMENT_JPEG_MASK 0x1
/* VB additional information MOTION DATA MASK */
#define OT_VB_SUPPLEMENT_MOTION_DATA_MASK 0x2
/* VB additional information DNG MASK */
#define OT_VB_SUPPLEMENT_DNG_MASK         0x4
/* VB additional information MISC MASK */
#define OT_VB_SUPPLEMENT_MISC_MASK        0x8
/* VB additional information BNR MOT MASK */
#define OT_VB_SUPPLEMENT_BNR_MOT_MASK   0x10
/* MMZ name length */
#define OT_MAX_MMZ_NAME_LEN             32
/* Define the macro ATTRIBUTE that defines the first address of the variable aligned by 8Bytes */
#define OT_ALIGN_NUM 8
#define ATTRIBUTE __attribute__((aligned (OT_ALIGN_NUM)))
/* Define the macro COMPAT_POINTER that clears the high 32 bits of pointer variables */
#define OT_COMPAT_POINTER(ptr, type) \
    do { \
        td_u32 dest_addr_ = (td_u32) (td_uintptr_t)ptr; \
        ptr = (type)( td_uintptr_t) dest_addr_; \
    } while (0)
```

**Notes**

The maximum number of video cache pools.

#### ot\_rotation\_view\_type<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659153"></a>

**Description**

Defines the rotational view mode.

**Definition**

```
typedef enum {
   OT_ROTATION_VIEW_TYPE_ALL      = 0, /* View all source Image,no lose*/
   OT_ROTATION_VIEW_TYPE_TYPICAL  = 1, /* View from rotation Image with source size,same lose*/
   OT_ROTATION_VIEW_TYPE_INSIDE   = 2, /* View with no black section,all  in dest Image*/
   OT_ROTATION_VIEW_TYPE_BUTT,
} ot_rotation_view_type;
```

</td>

</table>

**Notes**

Hi3403V100：

-   center\_x\_offsetValid range: \[-511, 511\]
-   center\_y\_offsetValid range: \[-511, 511\]; at the same time, the correction center point must fall within the image.
-   distortion\_ratioValid range: \[-300, 500\], where \[-300, 0\) corresponds to pincushion mode, and \[0, 500\] corresponds to barrel mode.

SS528V100/SS524V100/SS626V100 does not support this structure.

**Related Data Types and Interfaces**

None.

#### ot\_ldc\_version<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408099942"></a>

**Description**

Define LDC version.

**Definition**

```
typedef enum  {
    OT_LDC_V1 = 1,
    OT_LDC_V2 = 2,
    OT_LDC_V3 = 3,
    OT_LDC_VERSION_BUTT
} ot_ldc_version;

#### ot\_mpp\_sys\_cfg<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408259786"></a>

**Description**

Define the MPPSystem ControlAttribute Structure.

**Definition**

```
typedef struct {
    td_u32 align;
} ot_mpp_sys_cfg;
```

**Members**

<a name="table537mcpsimp"></a>
<table><thead align="left"><tr id="row542mcpsimp"><th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="28.999999999999996%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.1"><p id="p544mcpsimp"><a name="p544mcpsimp"></a><a name="p544mcpsimp"></a>Member Name</p>
</th>
<th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="71%" id="mcps1.1.3.1.2"><p id="p546mcpsimp"><a name="p546mcpsimp"></a><a name="p546mcpsimp"></a>Description</p>
</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody><tr id="row548mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="28.999999999999996%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p550mcpsimp"><a name="p550mcpsimp"></a><a name="p550mcpsimp"></a>align</p>
</td>
When align is 0, stride alignment uses automatic mode, and the alignment requirements follow the system.
When align is non-0, the specified stride is used for alignment.
<p id="p554mcpsimp"><a name="p554mcpsimp"></a><a name="p554mcpsimp"></a>Static Attribute。</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Notes**

-   When align is 0, stride alignment uses automatic mode, and the alignment requirements follow the system.
-   When align is non-0, the specified stride is used for alignment.
-   When align is smaller than the stride alignment required by the chip, the system allocates buffers and calculates stride based on the stride alignment required by the chip.
-   When align is not equal to the stride alignment multiple required by the chip, the system allocates a buffer and calculates the stride alignment upward to the stride alignment multiple required by the chip.

Example: alignset to 48, when the chip requires 32 alignment, the system uses 64 alignment when allocating buffers and calculating stride.

**Related Data Types and Interfaces**

[ss\_mpi\_sys\_set\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441699005)

#### ot\_scale\_range\_type<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659297"></a>

**Description**

An enumeration type that defines the scaling range for VPSS and VGS.

**Definition**

```
typedef enum  {
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_0 = 0, /* scale range <   8/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_1, /* scale range >=  8/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_2, /* scale range >= 10/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_3, /* scale range >= 15/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_4, /* scale range >= 19/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_5, /* scale range >= 24/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_6, /* scale range >= 29/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_7, /* scale range >= 33/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_8, /* scale range >= 35/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_9, /* scale range >= 38/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_10, /* scale range >= 42/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_11, /* scale range >= 45/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_12, /* scale range >= 48/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_13, /* scale range >= 51/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_14, /* scale range >= 53/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_15, /* scale range >= 55/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_16, /* scale range >= 57/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_17, /* scale range >= 60/64 */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_18, /* scale range >  1     */
    OT_SCALE_RANGE_BUTT,
} ot_scale_range_type;
```

**Members**

none

none

#### ot\_vb\_pool\_info<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659205"></a>

**Description**

Define the video buffer pool information structure.

**Definition**

```
typedef struct {
    td_u32 blk_cnt;
    td_u64 blk_size;
    td_u64 pool_size;
    td_phys_addr_t pool_phy_addr;
    ot_vb_remap_mode remap_mode;
} ot_vb_pool_info;
```

<p id="p6041mcpsimp"><a name="p6041mcpsimp"></a><a name="p6041mcpsimp"></a> Reserved, currently the internal fixed value is OT_VB_MAX_POOLS. </p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row6043mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="30%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p6045mcpsimp"><a name="p6045mcpsimp"></a><a name="p6045mcpsimp"></a>common_pool</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="70%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p6047mcpsimp"><a name="p6047mcpsimp"></a><a name="p6047mcpsimp"></a>Public cache pool Attribute Structure. </p>
<p id="p6048mcpsimp"><a name="p6048mcpsimp"></a><a name="p6048mcpsimp"></a>Static Attribute。</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Notes**

-   If blk\_size is equal to 0 or blk\_cnt is equal to 0, the corresponding buffer pool will not be created.
-   It is recommended that the entire structure be memset to 0 first and then assigned values ​​as needed.

**Related Data Types and Interfaces**

[ss\_mpi\_vb\_set\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659277)

</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row3888mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="24%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p3890mcpsimp"><a name="p3890mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3890mcpsimp"></a>blk_cnt</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="76%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p3892mcpsimp"><a name="p3892mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3892mcpsimp"></a>The number of cache blocks in each cache pool. </p>
<p id="p3893mcpsimp"><a name="p3893mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3893mcpsimp"></a>Valid range: [0, 10240]</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row3894mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="24%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p3896mcpsimp"><a name="p3896mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3896mcpsimp"></a>remap_mode</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="76%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p3903mcpsimp"><a name="p3903mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3903mcpsimp"></a>The MMZ area from which the current cache pool allocates memory. </p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row3899mcpsimp"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="24%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.1 "><p id="p3901mcpsimp"><a name="p3901mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3901mcpsimp"></a>mmz_name</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="76%" headers="mcps1.1.3.1.2 "><p id="p3903mcpsimp"><a name="p3903mcpsimp"></a><a name="p3903mcpsimp"></a>The MMZ area from which the current cache pool allocates memory. </p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Notes**

-   The size blk\_size of each cache block should be calculated based on the current image width and height, Pixel format, data bit width, whether to compress, etc. For detailed calculation methods, see Table 1 and the image storage calculation formulas of various formats in the code ot\_buffer.h.
-   When VB is allocated, 256 alignment operation will be performed on blk\_size.
-   The buffer pool is allocated from free MMZ memory. A buffer pool contains multiple buffer blocks of the same size. If the size of the cache pool exceeds the free space in reserved memory, creating the cache pool will fail.
-   The user needs to ensure that the entered DDR name already exists. If the entered DDR name does not exist, the memory will not be allocated. If the array mmz\_name is memset to 0, it means creating a buffer pool in an unnamed DDR.
-   Users need to ensure that the entered blk\_cnt range is legal. If the entered blk\_cnt exceeds the range, creating the cache pool will fail.

**Related Data Types and Interfaces**

-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_set\_cfg](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002441659277)
-   [ss\_mpi\_vb\_create\_pool](#ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000002408100006)
